It just feels all turned around.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or losing interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most mornings I wake up with this heavy, sad feeling, like it’s just sitting on me. Even when I’m in my kitchen and the coffee’s going and it’s bright in there, it still feels like the warmth has kind of gone out of everything. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling more tired than usual or like you don’t have the energy to get through the day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have, though I don’t want to overstate it. Even if I’ve had a full night, like when my aunt doesn’t call out or need anything, I’m still worn down by breakfast. Little things—carrying laundry, making phone calls—take more out of me than they should. I keep thinking I ought to be able to push through it, but… it’s been harder.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, feeling restless or fidgety in a way that’s unusual for you?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little slower, yes. I don’t mean to be difficult—I just sort of… need a moment before the words come.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty or like you’re not worth much, even about things that wouldn’t have bothered you before?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling more tired than usual or like you don’t have the energy to get through the day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have, though I don’t want to overstate it. Even if I’ve had a full night, like when my aunt doesn’t call out or need anything, I’m still worn down by breakfast. Little things—carrying laundry, making phone calls—take more out of me than they should. I keep thinking I ought to be able to push through it, but… it’s been harder.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, feeling restless or fidgety in a way that’s unusual for you?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little slower, yes. I didn’t really think it was noticeable, but… people have asked if my hip is bothering me because I’m moving so slowly through the grocery store. And at meetings, I guess I take longer to answer even simple questions. I don’t mean to be difficult—I just sort of… need a moment before the words come.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty or like you’re not worth much, even about things that wouldn’t have bothered you before?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I mean, I wouldn’t say I feel worthless exactly. That sounds so dramatic, and I don’t want to make it sound like I’m feeling sorry for myself. I guess I do second-guess myself more than I used to, but… guilt? I’m not sure. Maybe just normal regret over things, not anything unusual. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to think clearly, focus, or make decisions lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think so, not in a big way. I mean, I can still follow things and make decisions when I need to. Sometimes I take a bit longer, but maybe that’s just me being tired or overthinking it. I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Is that what you mean by trouble focusing?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that activities you used to enjoy, like hobbies, time with friends, or other things, just don’t feel as rewarding or interesting to you anymore?\n\nPatient: A little, I guess. I mean, I still try to do things, but… I used to get excited about picking out quilting squares, or bowling night, or planning the potlucks at church. Now I’ll look at my supplies, or the church calendar, and I just… can’t make myself care the way I used to. I don’t know if that counts, but it feels different.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently, like losing or gaining a lot without trying?\n\nPatient: Not a huge amount, I don’t think… I mean, I haven’t been keeping close track of the scale. But my eating has been kind of… off, for a few months now. Some days I’ll just pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’ll find myself in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really want. It sounds silly when I say it, but that’s part of why I came in, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live or that life isn’t worth living anymore?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think so. Not like wanting to hurt myself or anything. Sometimes I get tired of feeling this way, if that makes sense, but I wouldn’t say I don’t want to live. I hope that’s the kind of thing you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been persistently low or sad for most of the day, nearly every day, over the past few weeks or months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days I wake up with this heavy, sad feeling kind of sitting on me. Even when I’m in my kitchen and it’s bright, and the coffee’s going, it feels like the warmth has gone out of everything. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends, no longer feel pleasurable or rewarding to you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I would say that. I don’t want to make too much of it, but I used to really look forward to things like quilting squares, and bowling night, and helping plan potlucks at church. Now I’ll look at my supplies or see something on the church calendar and just… I can’t seem to care the way I used to. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don’t mean to be difficult—I just sort of… need a moment before the words come.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty or like you’re not worth much, even about things that wouldn’t have bothered you before?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I mean, I wouldn’t say I feel worthless exactly. That sounds so dramatic, and I don’t want to make it sound like I’m feeling sorry for myself. I guess I do second-guess myself more than I used to, but… guilt? I’m not sure. Maybe just normal regret over things, not anything unusual. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to think clearly, focus, or make decisions lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think so, not in a big way. I mean, I can still follow things and make decisions when I need to. Sometimes I take a bit longer, but maybe that’s just me being tired or overthinking it. I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Is that what you mean by trouble focusing?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that activities you used to enjoy, like hobbies, time with friends, or other things, just don’t feel as rewarding or interesting to you anymore?\n\nPatient: A little, I guess. I mean, I still try to do things, but… I used to get excited about picking out quilting squares, or bowling night, or planning the potlucks at church. Now I’ll look at my supplies, or the church calendar, and I just… can’t make myself care the way I used to. I don’t know if that counts, but it feels different.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently, like losing or gaining a lot without trying?\n\nPatient: Not a huge amount, I don’t think… I mean, I haven’t been keeping close track of the scale. But my eating has been kind of… off, for a few months now. Some days I’ll just pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’ll find myself in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really want. It sounds silly when I say it, but that’s part of why I came in, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live or that life isn’t worth living anymore?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think so. Not like wanting to hurt myself or anything. Sometimes I get tired of feeling this way, if that makes sense, but I wouldn’t say I don’t want to live. I hope that’s the kind of thing you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been persistently low or sad for most of the day, nearly every day, over the past few weeks or months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days I wake up with this heavy, sad feeling kind of sitting on me. Even when I’m in my kitchen and it’s bright, and the coffee’s going, it feels like the warmth has gone out of everything. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends, no longer feel pleasurable or rewarding to you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I would say that. I don’t want to make too much of it, but I used to really look forward to things like quilting squares, and bowling night, and helping plan potlucks at church. Now I’ll look at my supplies or see something on the church calendar and just… I can’t seem to care the way I used to. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight that are affecting your health or daily life?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I don’t think it’s anything too terrible, but my eating has gotten kind of turned around for months now. Some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’ll find myself standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. It feels embarrassing to say that. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of change you mean.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the low mood and changes in appetite and interest in activities?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s been about seven months now. It started around when my aunt came to live with me after she had her fall. I kept thinking I was just adjusting and it would pass, so I didn’t really count it at first, but looking back, that’s about when all this began.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess… mostly my eating has been kind of off, and it’s been that way for months. I don’t know if that’s the right thing to come in for, but some days I barely want more than toast and coffee, and then other days I’ll find myself buying sweets in the bakery aisle that I don’t even really enjoy. It just feels all turned around.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or losing interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most mornings I wake up with this heavy, sad feeling, like it’s just sitting on me. Even when I’m in my kitchen and the coffee’s going and it’s bright in there, it still feels like the warmth has kind of gone out of everything. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling more tired than usual or like you don’t have the energy to get through the day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have, though I don’t want to overstate it. Even if I’ve had a full night, like when my aunt doesn’t call out or need anything, I’m still worn down by breakfast. Little things—carrying laundry, making phone calls—take more out of me than they should. I keep thinking I ought to be able to push through it, but… it’s been harder.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, feeling restless or fidgety in a way that’s unusual for you?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little slower, yes. I didn’t really think it was noticeable, but… people have asked if my hip is bothering me because I’m moving so slowly through the grocery store. And at meetings, I guess I take longer to answer even simple questions. I don’t mean to be difficult—I just sort of… need a moment before the words come.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty or like you’re not worth much, even about things that wouldn’t have bothered you before?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I mean, I wouldn’t say I feel worthless exactly. That sounds so dramatic, and I don’t want to make it sound like I’m feeling sorry for myself. I guess I do second-guess myself more than I used to, but… guilt? I’m not sure. Maybe just normal regret over things, not anything unusual. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to think clearly, focus, or make decisions lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think so, not in a big way. I mean, I can still follow things and make decisions when I need to. Sometimes I take a bit longer, but maybe that’s just me being tired or overthinking it. I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Is that what you mean by trouble focusing?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that activities you used to enjoy, like hobbies, time with friends, or other things, just don’t feel as rewarding or interesting to you anymore?\n\nPatient: A little, I guess. I mean, I still try to do things, but… I used to get excited about picking out quilting squares, or bowling night, or planning the potlucks at church. But my eating has been kind of… off, for a few months now. Some days I’ll just pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’ll find myself in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really want. It sounds silly when I say it, but that’s part of why I came in, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live or that life isn’t worth living anymore?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think so. Yes… I mean, I don’t want to sound dramatic, but yes, kind of. I used to really look forward to things like quilting squares, bowling night, helping plan potlucks… I’d get excited about the colors or who was bringing what.

    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.1, F32.2, F33.1, F33.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess… mostly my eating has been kind of off, and it’s been that way for months. I don’t know if that’s the right thing to come in for, but some days I barely want more than toast and coffee, and then other days I’ll find myself buying sweets in the bakery aisle that I don’t even really enjoy. It just feels all turned around.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or losing interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most mornings I wake up with this heavy, sad feeling, like it’s just sitting on me. Even when I’m in my kitchen and the coffee’s going and it’s bright in there, it still feels like the warmth has kind of gone out of everything. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling more tired than usual or like you don’t have the energy to get through the day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have, though I don’t want to overstate it. Even if I’ve had a full night, like when my aunt doesn’t call out or need anything, I’m still worn down by breakfast. Little things—carrying laundry, making phone calls—take more out of me than they should. I keep thinking I ought to be able to push through it, but… it’s been harder.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, feeling restless or fidgety in a way that’s unusual for you?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little slower, yes. I didn’t really think it was noticeable, but… people have asked if my hip is bothering me because I’m moving so slowly through the grocery store. And at meetings, I guess I take longer to answer even simple questions. I don’t mean to be difficult—I just sort of… need a moment before the words come.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty or like you’re not worth much, even about things that wouldn’t have bothered you before?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I mean, I wouldn’t say I feel worthless exactly. That sounds so dramatic, and I don’t want to make it sound like I’m feeling sorry for myself. I guess I do second-guess myself more than I used to, but… guilt? I’m not sure. Maybe just normal regret over things, not anything unusual. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to think clearly, focus, or make decisions lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think so, not in a big way. I mean, I can still follow things and make decisions when I need to. Sometimes I take a bit longer, but maybe that’s just me being tired or overthinking it. I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Is that what you mean by trouble focusing?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that activities you used to enjoy, like hobbies, time with friends, or other things, just don’t feel as rewarding or interesting to you anymore?\n\nPatient: A little, I guess. I mean, I still try to do things, but… I used to get excited about picking out quilting squares, or bowling night, or planning the potlucks at church. Now I’ll look at my supplies, or the church calendar, and I just… can’t make myself care the way I used to. I don’t know if that counts, but it feels different.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently, like losing or gaining a lot without trying?\n\nPatient: Not a huge amount, I don’t think… I mean, I haven’t been keeping close track of the scale. But my eating has been kind of… off, for a few months now. Some days I’ll just pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’ll find myself in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really want. It sounds silly when I say it, but that’s part of why I came in, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live or that life isn’t worth living anymore?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think so. Not like wanting to hurt myself or anything. Sometimes I get tired of feeling this way, if that makes sense, but I wouldn’t say I don’t want to live. I hope that’s the kind of thing you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been persistently low or sad for most of the day, nearly every day, over the past few weeks or months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days I wake up with this heavy, sad feeling kind of sitting on me. Even when I’m in my kitchen and it’s bright, and the coffee’s going, it feels like the warmth has gone out of everything. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends, no longer feel pleasurable or rewarding to you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I would say that. I don’t want to make too much of it, but I used to really look forward to things like quilting squares, and bowling night, and helping plan potlucks at church. Now I’ll look at my supplies or see something on the church calendar and just… I can’t seem to care the way I used to. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight that are affecting your health or daily life?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I don’t think it’s anything too terrible, but my eating has gotten kind of turned around for months now. Some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’ll find myself standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. It feels embarrassing to say that. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of change you mean.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the low mood and changes in appetite and interest in activities?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s been about seven months now. It started around when my aunt came to live with me after she had her fall. I haven’t been trying to lose weight or anything… I’m not sure if that answers it right.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you completely lost your appetite or didn’t eat for entire days because you weren’t interested in food at all?\n\nPatient: Not for an entire day, I don’t think… at least not that I can remember. Not like wanting to hurt myself or anything. Sometimes I get tired of feeling this way, if that makes sense, but I wouldn’t say I don’t want to live. I hope that’s the kind of thing you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been persistently low or sad for most of the day, nearly every day, over the past few weeks or months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days I wake up with this heavy, sad feeling kind of sitting on me. Even when I’m in my kitchen and it’s bright, and the coffee’s going, it feels like the warmth has gone out of everything. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends, no longer feel pleasurable or rewarding to you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I would say that. I don’t want to make too much of it, but I used to really look forward to things like quilting squares, and bowling night, and helping plan potlucks at church. Now I’ll look at my supplies or see something on the church calendar and just… I can’t seem to care the way I used to. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight that are affecting your health or daily life?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I don’t think it’s anything too terrible, but my eating has gotten kind of turned around for months now. Some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’ll find myself standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. It feels embarrassing to say that. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of change you mean.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the low mood and changes in appetite and interest in activities?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s been about seven months now. It started around when my aunt came to live with me after she had her fall. I kept thinking I was just adjusting and it would pass, so I didn’t really count it at first, but looking back, that’s about when all this began.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself losing weight without trying, or has there been a time when you completely lost your appetite for days at a time?\n\nPatient: Not completely for days, no… not exactly. It’s more that my eating has just been all turned around for months. Some days I’ll barely pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I haven’t been trying to lose weight or anything… I’m not sure if that answers it right.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you completely lost your appetite or didn’t eat for entire days because you weren’t interested in food at all?\n\nPatient: Not for an entire day, I don’t think… at least not that I can remember. It’s more like I’ll get through on just toast and coffee, sort of picking at it, and then another day I’ll be buying sweets in the bakery aisle even though I don’t really want them. It’s been like that for months. I’m sorry, I don’t know if that’s quite what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve completely lost interest in activities you used to enjoy, like quilting or church potlucks, to the point where they feel meaningless or uninteresting to you now?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I don’t want to sound dramatic, but yes, kind of. I used to really look forward to things like quilting squares, bowling night, helping plan potlucks… I’d get excited about the colors or who was bringing what.\n\nNow I’ll look at my quilting supplies or see something on the church calendar, and I just… can’t make myself care like I used to. It feels flat, I guess. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that nothing gives you pleasure anymore—not quilting, not seeing friends, not even small things like a good cup of coffee or a sunny day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say nothing at all… that sounds so absolute. But the things I used to really look forward to, like quilting squares, bowling night, helping with potlucks—I just don’t feel much about them now.\n\nI’ll see my quilting things sitting there, or look at the church calendar, and I know I should care, or I used to care… but I can’t quite make myself feel interested the way I did. Is that what you mean?"
